references:
- citation: 'I. Dolgachev, S. Kondo, "The rationality of the moduli spaces of Coble surfaces and of nodal Enriques surfaces", Izv. Math. 77:3 (2013), 507-524, Section 2, p. 510: the conventions ($\mathbb{E} = E_8 \oplus U$ with $E_8$ the even negative definite lattice of the Cartan matrix, $L(2)$ the form $2b$, $\langle m \rangle$ the rank-one lattice of a vector of norm $m$, $A_L = L^*/L$ with its quadratic form $q_L$), and Section 3, equation (3.6) with Proposition 3.2, p. 513: the Coble lattice is $N = \langle 2 \rangle \oplus \mathbb{E}(2) = \langle 2 \rangle \oplus U(2) \oplus E_8(-2)$, of signature $(2, 9)$, and the moduli space of Coble surfaces is an open subset of $\mathcal{D}(N)/O(N)$.'
---

The Coble lattice $T_{\mathrm{Co}} = \langle 2 \rangle \oplus U(2) \oplus E_8(-2)$, in the basis $e_{0}$ of the first summand, $e_{1}, f_{1}$ of $U(2)$ with $b(e_{1}, f_{1}) = 2$, and the simple roots of $E_{8}(-2)$: rank $11$, signature $(2, 9)$, determinant $-2048$, even.

$T_{\mathrm{Co}}$ is twice the odd unimodular lattice $I_{2,9} = \langle 1 \rangle \oplus U \oplus E_{8}(-1)$, so $T_{\mathrm{Co}} \cong I_{2,9}(2)$; $b(e_{1}, e_{1}) = 0$, so it is isotropic.
Its discriminant group is $A_{T_{\mathrm{Co}}} = (T_{\mathrm{Co}})^{*}/T_{\mathrm{Co}}$, of order $2048$.
The groups studied with it are the discriminant kernel $\widetilde O(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ and $\Gamma_{\mathrm{Co}} = \widetilde O^{+}(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$, which have their own arithmetic-group cards `otilde-t-co` and `otilde-plus-t-co`.

No invariant of this card is transcribed from a source: `latticedb certify` computes each from the Gram tensor above and certifies it, so a field is absent until its computation runs.
$E_8$ is negative definite, in Dolgachev–Kondo's convention for $\mathbb{E}(2) = (E_8 \oplus U)(2)$ and in the Gram tensor above, whose third summand has $-4$ on its diagonal and $-2$ off it.

`integral.primitive_orbits` states, for each of the eight standard subgroups, the constant term: the number of $\Gamma$-orbits of primitive isotropic *lines*, the $0$-cusps of the Baily–Borel boundary, and not a count of vectors (`theory/orbits.md`).
The preamble returns it for an indefinite lattice as the constant term of `primitive_orbit_series`, which is `isotropic_orbit_representatives(1).cardinality()` of each subgroup; on an indefinite lattice nothing positive or negative is bounded, so the $z$ and $w$ coefficients are empty.
The count is what the operation returned for the stored Gram tensor: no page's count is transcribed, and the observed isotropic classes of $A_{T_{\mathrm{Co}}}$ are not an orbit count.
All eight rows are stated: $O$, $SO$, $O^{+}$, $SO^{+}$ are $1$ and $\widetilde{O}$, $S\widetilde{O}$, $\widetilde{O}^{+}$, $S\widetilde{O}^{+}$ are $527$, each the operation's answer for that subgroup.
$S\widetilde{O}^{+}$ arrived last and it arrived: the row was quarantined in `failures/e1.yaml` (`6JRR integral.primitive_orbits[SOtilde+]`) while its line-orbit walk did not return on this lattice, the owner then certified the stabilizer's sign space from the stabilizer's own exhibited elements (sage-extensions#596, `dbf91e89`), the row was released through `just retry-computation`, and this card's computation returned $527$ for the stored Gram tensor — the same count as the three subgroups above it, so the determinant and spinor restrictions split no primitive isotropic line here.
The certificate over this block commits to all eight rows, and it is one certificate for the field rather than one per row.
`latticedb verify --shard 1/16` — the shard this tag falls in — passed this card, both arithmetic-group cards and every card they name: $18910$ cards at a $3.6$ GiB peak, no problem on any of the three. The shard's own twelve problems are `definite.regular` and `definite.spinor_regular` contradictions on seven other definite cards, and are theirs.

`planes` on a row is the same subgroup's count of its orbits of primitive isotropic **planes**, the $1$-cusps of the Baily–Borel boundary. It is a computation of the row and not a second row, and a run asks for it by naming the part — `latticedb certify --field 'primitive_orbits[O].planes'` — or by naming this card and no field, `latticedb certify --tag 6JRR`, which asks every part this card does not state; the sharded nightly names no card and asks none of them, because it is the owner's plane walk and not its line walk: rows $O$, $O^{+}$ and $\widetilde O^{+}$ state $1$, $1$ and $189720$, which this card's computation returned for the stored Gram tensor — $O$ in $208.4$ s at a $546$ MiB peak, $O^{+}$ by the owner's odd-stabilizer route, which decides a sign-only subgroup's row from one witness instead of walking the ambient classification, and $\widetilde O^{+}$ by the parabolic coset walk in $2000$ s at a $3.3$ GiB peak — the ambient group being transitive on the primitive isotropic planes, so the kernel group's orbits are the split of one ambient orbit.
The two determinant-cutting rows are **refused by the owner**, and the refusal is why they are empty rather than unasked. `O.SO().cusps(2)` and `(O_plus() & SO()).cusps(2)` raise in `sage_extensions/categories/orthogonal_quotients.py:4237`, `_plane_orbit_from_parabolic_stabilizer`: "the plane row of ... under {g in O(T_Co) : det(g)=1} cannot be computed by the coset walk of the discriminant image: the subgroup cuts no discriminant condition, or both a determinant and a spinor kernel at once, and the walk's kernel-sign witness machinery lifts one sign coset only". The cause is that the route which decides a sign-only subgroup's plane row, `_plane_orbit_from_odd_stabilizer`, is restricted to the spinor kernel alone (`spinor_kernel and not determinant_kernel and not _has_discriminant`), so $O^{+}$ is answered there and $SO$ and $SO^{+}$ fall to the parabolic route, which refuses them. That is an owner boundary and not a property of the lattice, so it is named in `results/issue-286-so-plane-row-refusal.md` instead of being worked around here; the two rows stay empty, and the failure ledger holds both refusals with the owner's traceback.
The four kernel rows state no plane count yet and no value is transcribed for them. Their route is the owner's parabolic coset walk, and the guard that walk reads, `subgroup.finite_character_quotient()` with its three flags, answers for three of the four: $\widetilde O$ has a discriminant condition and no sign kernel (index $46998591897600$), $S\widetilde O$ adds the determinant kernel (index $93997183795200$) and $\widetilde O^{+}$ the spinor kernel (same index), and the guard refuses none of them. $S\widetilde O^{+}$, which cuts the two sign kernels at once, is not reachable here at all: the character quotient itself, alone in a fresh process, was killed after $594$ s, and an earlier measurement at the #596 epoch saw the subgroup pass $9.2$ GiB after $510$ s and keep growing, so the row is quarantined in `failures/e1.yaml` with that measurement and no run asks it.
The expected values are proved in research, on `main`: `thm:coble-plane-orbit-counts` gives one orbit under each of $O$, $SO$, $O^{+}$, $SO^{+}$ and $189720$ under the four kernel subgroups, which is the plane column of `cor:coble-eight-standard-line-counts`; the records behind the instrument are sage-extensions#286 (`results/issue286-the-stage-1-call.md`) and its image run `44392429173`, which reports $189720$ plane cusps for $\widetilde O(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ and for $\widetilde O^{+}(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ and the $569160$ incidence edges between the two strata. A comparison is not a card value, $\widetilde O^{+}$'s part arrived: $189720$, the count the theorem gives and the count image run `44392429173` reports for that group. The four kernel rows have one plane count between them, and that is decided rather than assumed: no sign condition splits $K$'s plane orbits, because the stabilizer of the standard primitive isotropic plane inside $K$ contains the three diagonal sign isometries that negate the $\langle 2 \rangle$ summand, the $U(2)$ summand and the $E_8(-2)$ summand, whose $ (\det, \mathrm{sn}_{\mathbb{R}}) $ pairs $(−1,−1)$, $(+1,−1)$ and $(+1,+1)$ generate $\{\pm1\}^2$; $O(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ is transitive on the primitive isotropic planes, so conjugation carries them to every plane (Dolgachev–Kondo, §2 p. 510 and §3 (3.6) with Proposition 3.2, p. 513 for the lattice and its discriminant kernel; Dutour Sikirić–Hulek, Lemma 3.6, p. 22 for the reduction through the finite image). `results/verify-7-plane-sign-witnesses.py` exhibits the three on the stored Gram tensor with the owner's membership predicates, and `results/evidence-7-plane-rows.md` records the table. So a kernel row's plane count, once its walk returns, is $189720$ — and $S\widetilde O^{+}$'s row stays empty because that subgroup's character quotient does not build here, not because its count is in doubt.
The incidence of the two strata is fixed the same way: the relation between $K$'s line and plane cusps is $(1080,3)$-biregular, so it has $527 \cdot 1080 = 189720 \cdot 3 = 569160$ edges and $K$ has $569160$ isotropic-flag orbits — $1080 = 135 \cdot 8$ is counted on this card's own discriminant form — $23715 = 142290/6$ order-four isotropic subgroups, $135$ of them through a fixed nonzero isotropic class, times the eight stars of a subgroup, and that eight is not a cited number here either: a plane cusp is a pair $(J,s)$ with $s$ a star of $J$ (Dutour Sikirić–Hulek, Lemma 3.6, printed p. 22), so the stars per subgroup are $189720/23715 = 8$ exactly, the quotient of this card's computed plane count by the subgroups counted on its discriminant form (`results/probe-7-star-count.py`) — and $3$ is the three nonzero classes of a plane's $J(P) = \mathrm{im}(P/2) \cong (\mathbb{Z}/2)^2$, which index three distinct line cusps because the line cusps are indexed by the nonzero isotropic classes of $A_{T_{\mathrm{Co}}}$ (Dolgachev–Kondo, §2 p. 510 and §3 (3.6) with Proposition 3.2, p. 513, for the lattice and its discriminant kernel). That $3$ is **not** three isotropic lines inside one plane, and this card is the place to say so: in this basis the plane is a scaled hyperbolic plane, $(a e_2 + b e_3)^2 = 4ab$, so its only primitive isotropic lines are the two coordinate ones — measured on the stored Gram in `results/probe-7-plane-lines.py` — while $J(P)$ has three nonzero classes, and the line realizing the third lies in another plane of the same $K$-orbit. `results/probe-flag-arithmetic.py` closes that arithmetic against this card's own $527$ and $189720$. That the edge count is the flag-orbit count needs one more step — distinct flag orbits must have distinct endpoint pairs — and it is tested rather than assumed where both sides are computable: on $T_{\mathrm{En}}$'s stored Gram under $O(T_{\mathrm{En}})$, two line cusps and two plane cusps give $3$ incidence edges and $3$ isotropic-flag orbits, the two counts returned by different owner calls (`results/probe-7-injectivity-specimen.py`). Neither group card's `flag_orbits` is stored, because that count is the incidence walk and no walk has returned.
The other two reachable parts are still empty, and `certify` with `tags=6JRR` asks for them — `primitive_orbits[Otilde].planes` and `primitive_orbits[SOtilde].planes` — with the two group cards' plane and flag counts.
Two local attempts at the first of them measured the same stop twice: $\widetilde O$'s plane walk ran $677$ s and then $1054$ s to a $1236606976$ B and a $1065508864$ B worker peak, $S\widetilde O$'s first attempt ran $566$ s at a $1130909696$ B peak, and each run ended the worker at its $1676462899$ B reserve floor while other projects held this machine at between $1.5$ and $2.9$ GiB available; the card was left as it was both times, and both rows were released with `just retry-computation` so a dispatched run still asks them. The owner's own record measured the row at $3476$ MiB and $2171.0$ s on a runner of its own, which is the headroom this VM does not have.

The arithmetic-group cards attached to this lattice are `otilde-t-co`, the discriminant kernel $\widetilde O(T_{\mathrm{Co}}) = \ker(O(T_{\mathrm{Co}}) \to O(A_{T_{\mathrm{Co}}}))$, and `otilde-plus-t-co`, its intersection with the kernel $O^{+}(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ of the real spinor norm — not with the determinant-one subgroup, which is $SO$.
Dolgachev–Kondo, Proposition 3.2, p. 513, describes $\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Co}}$ as an open subset of $\mathcal{D}(N)/O(N)$; that the group acting on this period domain is the image of $\widetilde O^{+}(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$, which the literature writes $\Gamma_{\mathrm{Co}}$, is a conjecture and not a result proved here or by that proposition, so it settles no count on this card.
Both arithmetic-group cards now store **proved ordinary integral generating families**, as self-isometries of this lattice: 56 generators for $\widetilde O(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$ and 95 Reidemeister--Schreier generators for $\widetilde O^+(T_{\mathrm{Co}})$. Their source-hashed exact matrices, full infinite-generation proofs, and public membership regressions are in research#667, theorems `thm:coble-fifty-six-ordinary-full-kernel-generation` and `thm:coble-positive-kernel-ninety-five-generators`. The group cards list the stored morphism names; their generator fields are independently certified on this card. The historical failure of a 46-trillion-coset Schreier enumeration does not invalidate the completed alternative proof.

The incidence of the two strata — the orbits of isotropic flags, one primitive isotropic line inside one primitive isotropic plane — is a field of these two group cards, `flag_orbits`, which the schema stores only beside the `line_orbits` and `plane_orbits` it joins.
Each count is asked for by its own name and read from the instrument that is cheap for its rank: `--field arithmetic-group.otilde-t-co.line_orbits` asks the subgroup's gathered `isotropic_orbit_representatives(1)`, the row the lattice card's own line count uses, and `--field …plane_orbits` asks its retained cusp row `g.cusps(2)`; naming `flag_orbits` asks those two with it, because a flag count is stored only beside the counts it joins, and naming a line count does not buy the plane walk.
Both group cards state their line count: $527$ each, this card's computation for the stored Gram tensor, certified under `arithmetic-group.otilde-t-co.line_orbits` and `arithmetic-group.otilde-plus-t-co.line_orbits`. That is the lattice card's own row read through the subgroup, and the theorem's $527$ lines as well.
Their plane counts and the incidence remain unstated: the two kernel incidence rows returned in $6132.2$ s and $7973.4$ s in image run `44392429173`, the plane rows are the 189720-class walks this card names above, and no count is written before the walk that produced it.
The comparison they will be read against is research `cor:coble-eight-standard-line-counts`: $527$ lines, $189720$ planes and $569160$ flags for $\widetilde O$ and for $\widetilde O^{+}$, the incidence being $(1080,3)$-biregular because $527 \cdot 1080 = 189720 \cdot 3 = 569160$, and $1$, $1$ and $1$ for each of $O$, $SO$, $O^{+}$ and $SO^{+}$.
